Drinking more… I don’t know. I’ll have a beer now and then, same as a lot of guys, especially after a long day. But I haven’t really been keeping score or anything.

That’s not really like me, so… yeah, that’s what pushed me to come talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it depressed, exactly. I mean, everybody gets a little worn down, right? Especially with work being the way it is — long days, people needing things yesterday, traffic backed up for no reason. \n\nBut down like… sad all the time? I’m not sure. I haven’t really stopped to think of it that way. I’ve mostly just been trying to keep up with things.\n\nDoctor: Have you had this kind of trouble paying attention since you were a child?\n\nPatient: No, not really since I was a kid. I mean, I wasn’t the best student or anything — high school was high school, you know, teachers always had their way of doing things — but this kind of trouble feels more recent.\n\nI’d say it’s been maybe… seven months or so. Around when my daughter moved back home. Since then I’ve been trying to keep everything steady, you know, work and home and all that, and that’s when I started noticing I wasn’t tracking things like I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Worrying a lot… I don’t know. I mean, I think about stuff, sure. Work stuff, fuel, whether a customer’s gonna be annoyed if something’s late — but that’s kind of the job, you know?\n\nLike yesterday I was sitting there looking at the gas prices and thinking how they used to be way lower, and then you start thinking about which routes make more sense, and then somebody on the CB is talking nonsense and it’s just… it piles up. But worry-worry? I’m not sure I’d call it that. It’s more like my head keeps going from one thing to the next.\n\nDoctor: Have you been drinking more alcohol lately?\n\nPatient: Drinking more… I don’t know. I’ll have a beer now and then, same as a lot of guys, especially after a long day. But I haven’t really been keeping score or anything.\n\nFunny thing is, some places you deliver to, there’s always a bar right nearby, and the same trucks parked out front every week. I see that and think, well, people have their routines. Me, I’m usually just trying to get home or beat the traffic. So… I’m not sure I’d say more lately.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times after drinking when you couldn’t remember what happened?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not like waking up somewhere and not knowing how I got there, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere’s been nights where I’m tired and I’ll forget where I put my keys or what I was gonna do next, but that happens after a long route too. My buddy used to joke that half the stuff we forget is just because we’re getting older, not because of beer. So… no, I haven’t noticed anything like that after drinking, not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Has your drinking ever caused problems at work or at home?\n\nPatient: At work or home? Not that I can really point to. I mean, I think about stuff, sure. Work stuff, fuel, whether a customer’s gonna be annoyed if something’s late — but that’s kind of the job, you know?\n\nLike yesterday I was sitting there looking at the gas prices and thinking how they used to be way lower, and then you start thinking about which routes make more sense, and then somebody on the CB is talking nonsense and it’s just… it piles up. But worry-worry? I’m not sure I’d call it that. It’s more like my head keeps going from one thing to the next.\n\nDoctor: Have you been drinking more alcohol lately?\n\nPatient: Drinking more… I don’t know. I’ll have a beer now and then, same as a lot of guys, especially after a long day. But I haven’t really been keeping score or anything.\n\nFunny thing is, some places you deliver to, there’s always a bar right nearby, and the same trucks parked out front every week. I see that and think, well, people have their routines. Me, I’m usually just trying to get home or beat the traffic. So… I’m not sure I’d say more lately.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times after drinking when you couldn’t remember what happened?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not like waking up somewhere and not knowing how I got there, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere’s been nights where I’m tired and I’ll forget where I put my keys or what I was gonna do next, but that happens after a long route too. My buddy used to joke that half the stuff we forget is just because we’re getting older, not because of beer. So… no, I haven’t noticed anything like that after drinking, not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Has your drinking ever caused problems at work or at home?\n\nPatient: At work or home? Not that I can really point to. I mean, my wife might say I’m annoying if I leave a bottle cap on the counter or something, but that’s not exactly a “problem,” you know?\n\nWork-wise, I don’t drink on the job. With driving, you can’t mess around with that. I’ve seen guys lose routes over stupid choices, and once your record’s got a mark on it, good luck getting decent work again. So… no, I don’t think drinking has caused trouble like that for me.\n\nDoctor: Over the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling down most days?\n\nPatient: Down most days… I don’t know if I’d put it like that. I mean, everybody gets worn out, right? Especially when you’re on the road and traffic’s backed up and dispatch is calling like you can magically move cars out of the way.\n\nThese last couple weeks, I’ve had some off days, sure, but I haven’t really been keeping a chart of my mood or anything. Sometimes I come home quiet, but sometimes I’m just tired. So… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed being down most days, exactly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… tense, or keyed up, I guess that fits more than “tense” exactly. It’s like I can’t just sit there. At home, my wife and daughter have been walking on eggshells, and I can tell I’m adding strain when I’m supposed to be helping.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 7 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about seven months, since my daughter moved back home and I started trying to keep everything steady.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 50 / driver_sales_worker_or_truck_driver / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S039\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about a lot of different things?\n\nPatient: Excessively… I don’t know. I mean, I worry about normal stuff, I guess—work, bills, whether the truck’s making that sound again, because one time I ignored a little rattle and it turned into a whole ordeal at a rest stop outside Toledo.\n\nBut “a lot of different things” like all the time? I’m not sure I’d say that. Sometimes my mind goes from one thing to another, but I don’t know if I’d call it worrying exactly. It’s more like… I notice something and then I’m on to the next thing. So, I guess I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or low a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Sad or low… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say that’s been the main thing, no. I mean, everybody has days where they’re worn out or kind of fed up—like if traffic’s backed up for no reason and you’re sitting there watching the clock, that’ll put anybody in a mood.\n\nBut sad a lot lately? I haven’t really thought of it that way. My wife might say I’m distracted or hard to pin down, but low… I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: On most days, do you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Anxious… on most days, I’m not sure I’d put that word on it. Like, some days I’ve got that wound-up feeling, sure, especially if there’s stuff that needs doing, but anxious makes me think of, I don’t know, sitting there scared something bad’s about to happen.\n\nI’m more just… moving around, thinking of the next thing. Like if I’m on a route and I know there’s construction near the depot, I’ll start figuring out three different ways around it, but that’s kind of just how you do the job, you know? So… I don’t really know. Maybe sometimes, but I haven’t been thinking “I’m anxious” most days.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been enjoying your usual activities less than you used to?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Enjoying things less… I don’t know, I haven’t really measured it like that. I still do the usual stuff when it comes up, you know? Watch a game if it’s on, mess around in the garage a bit, talk to the guys at work when there’s time.

Sometimes I get sidetracked before I even sit down to do something—like I’ll go out to clean up the workbench and then notice the leaf blower needs gas, and then I’m thinking about the mower belt, and next thing you know I didn’t actually do the thing I went out there for. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I guess what finally made me come in is I’ve been getting real scattered lately. Like, I’m out driving and my mind jumps at every little thing — CB chatter, a fuel price sign, some random idea I get — and then I miss stuff I normally wouldn’t.\n\nI’ve been doing this kind of work a long time, you know, deliveries and clients and keeping track of who needs what, and usually I’m pretty solid. But lately I’ll miss a delivery note or forget who I said I’d call back. The other day I was thinking about fuel prices — they’ve been all over the place, and everybody’s got an opinion about them — and then I realized I’d overlooked something on a client’s paperwork. That’s not really like me, so… yeah, that’s what pushed me to come talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it depressed, exactly. I mean, everybody gets a little worn down, right? Especially with work being the way it is — long days, people needing things yesterday, traffic backed up for no reason. \n\nBut down like… sad all the time? I’m not sure. I haven’t really stopped to think of it that way. I’ve mostly just been trying to keep up with things.\n\nDoctor: Have you had this kind of trouble paying attention since you were a child?\n\nPatient: No, not really since I was a kid. I mean, I wasn’t the best student or anything — high school was high school, you know, teachers always had their way of doing things — but this kind of trouble feels more recent.\n\nI’d say it’s been maybe… seven months or so. Around when my daughter moved back home. Since then I’ve been trying to keep everything steady, you know, work and home and all that, and that’s when I started noticing I wasn’t tracking things like I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Worrying a lot… I don’t know. I mean, I think about stuff, sure. Work stuff, fuel, whether a customer’s gonna be annoyed if something’s late — but that’s kind of the job, you know?\n\nLike yesterday I was sitting there looking at the gas prices and thinking how they used to be way lower, and then you start thinking about which routes make more sense, and then somebody on the CB is talking nonsense and it’s just… it piles up. But worry-worry? I’m not sure I’d call it that. It’s more like my head keeps going from one thing to the next.\n\nDoctor: Have you been drinking more alcohol lately?\n\nPatient: Drinking more… I don’t know. I’ll have a beer now and then, same as a lot of guys, especially after a long day. But I haven’t really been keeping score or anything.\n\nFunny thing is, some places you deliver to, there’s always a bar right nearby, and the same trucks parked out front every week. I see that and think, well, people have their routines. Me, I’m usually just trying to get home or beat the traffic. So… I’m not sure I’d say more lately.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times after drinking when you couldn’t remember what happened?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not like waking up somewhere and not knowing how I got there, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere’s been nights where I’m tired and I’ll forget where I put my keys or what I was gonna do next, but that happens after a long route too. My buddy used to joke that half the stuff we forget is just because we’re getting older, not because of beer. So… no, I haven’t noticed anything like that after drinking, not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Has your drinking ever caused problems at work or at home?\n\nPatient: At work or home? Not that I can really point to. I mean, my wife might say I’m annoying if I leave a bottle cap on the counter or something, but that’s not exactly a “problem,” you know?\n\nWork-wise, I don’t drink on the job. With driving, you can’t mess around with that. I’ve seen guys lose routes over stupid choices, and once your record’s got a mark on it, good luck getting decent work again. So… no, I don’t think drinking has caused trouble like that for me.\n\nDoctor: Over the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling down most days?\n\nPatient: Down most days… I don’t know if I’d put it like that. I mean, everybody gets worn out, right? Especially when you’re on the road and traffic’s backed up and dispatch is calling like you can magically move cars out of the way.\n\nThese last couple weeks, I’ve had some off days, sure, but I haven’t really been keeping a chart of my mood or anything. Sometimes I come home quiet, but sometimes I’m just tired. So… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed being down most days, exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel these concentration problems started because of the stress of your daughter moving back home?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it was because of her moving back in, exactly. I mean, that’s stressful, sure—anybody coming back into the house changes the whole rhythm, and then there’s groceries and noise and where everyone parks in the driveway...\n\nBut the thing that really got me to come in is, for months now, my mind just keeps jumping. Like I’ll be driving and every CB chirp, every fuel price sign, some random idea pops in my head, and I’m off track. Then I miss stuff I normally wouldn’t miss—like a client’s delivery note, or I forget who I told I’d call back. That’s not really like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tense or keyed up much of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… tense, or keyed up, I guess that fits more than “tense” exactly. It’s like I can’t just sit there. If I get home and sit on the couch, I start thinking, well, the route plans could be cleaned up, or maybe I should check on one of the newer guys and see if he figured out that dock entrance—because if you miss that turn, you end up circling forever, and I’ve done that before.\n\nAnd then at home I’ll start fixing little things, like a cabinet hinge or messing with something in the garage that didn’t need to be messed with right then. My wife will say, “Can you just sit down?” and I mean, I try, but I get antsy pretty quick. So yeah, I’ve been keeping myself busy with extra stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about a lot of different things?\n\nPatient: Excessively… I don’t know. I’m not sure I’d say that. Sometimes my mind goes from one thing to another, but I don’t know if I’d call it worrying exactly. It’s more like… I notice something and then I’m on to the next thing. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I guess what finally made me come in is I’ve been getting real scattered lately. Like, I’m out driving and my mind jumps at every little thing — CB chatter, a fuel price sign, some random idea I get — and then I miss stuff I normally wouldn’t.\n\nI’ve been doing this kind of work a long time, you know, deliveries and clients and keeping track of who needs what, and usually I’m pretty solid. But lately I’ll miss a delivery note or forget who I said I’d call back. The other day I was thinking about fuel prices — they’ve been all over the place, and everybody’s got an opinion about them — and then I realized I’d overlooked something on a client’s paperwork. That’s not really like me, so… yeah, that’s what pushed me to come talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it depressed, exactly. I mean, everybody gets a little worn down, right? Especially with work being the way it is — long days, people needing things yesterday, traffic backed up for no reason. \n\nBut down like… sad all the time? I’m not sure. I haven’t really stopped to think of it that way. I’ve mostly just been trying to keep up with things.\n\nDoctor: Have you had this kind of trouble paying attention since you were a child?\n\nPatient: No, not really since I was a kid. I mean, I wasn’t the best student or anything — high school was high school, you know, teachers always had their way of doing things — but this kind of trouble feels more recent.\n\nI’d say it’s been maybe… seven months or so. Around when my daughter moved back home. Since then I’ve been trying to keep everything steady, you know, work and home and all that, and that’s when I started noticing I wasn’t tracking things like I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Worrying a lot… I don’t know. I mean, I think about stuff, sure. Work stuff, fuel, whether a customer’s gonna be annoyed if something’s late — but that’s kind of the job, you know?\n\nLike yesterday I was sitting there looking at the gas prices and thinking how they used to be way lower, and then you start thinking about which routes make more sense, and then somebody on the CB is talking nonsense and it’s just… it piles up. But worry-worry? I’m not sure I’d call it that. It’s more like my head keeps going from one thing to the next.\n\nDoctor: Have you been drinking more alcohol lately?\n\nPatient: Drinking more… I don’t know. I’ll have a beer now and then, same as a lot of guys, especially after a long day. But I haven’t really been keeping score or anything.\n\nFunny thing is, some places you deliver to, there’s always a bar right nearby, and the same trucks parked out front every week. I see that and think, well, people have their routines. Me, I’m usually just trying to get home or beat the traffic. So… I’m not sure I’d say more lately.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times after drinking when you couldn’t remember what happened?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not like waking up somewhere and not knowing how I got there, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere’s been nights where I’m tired and I’ll forget where I put my keys or what I was gonna do next, but that happens after a long route too. My buddy used to joke that half the stuff we forget is just because we’re getting older, not because of beer. So… no, I haven’t noticed anything like that after drinking, not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Has your drinking ever caused problems at work or at home?\n\nPatient: At work or home? Not that I can really point to. I mean, my wife might say I’m annoying if I leave a bottle cap on the counter or something, but that’s not exactly a “problem,” you know?\n\nWork-wise, I don’t drink on the job. With driving, you can’t mess around with that. I’ve seen guys lose routes over stupid choices, and once your record’s got a mark on it, good luck getting decent work again. So… no, I don’t think drinking has caused trouble like that for me.\n\nDoctor: Over the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling down most days?\n\nPatient: Down most days… I don’t know if I’d put it like that. I mean, everybody gets worn out, right? Especially when you’re on the road and traffic’s backed up and dispatch is calling like you can magically move cars out of the way.\n\nThese last couple weeks, I’ve had some off days, sure, but I haven’t really been keeping a chart of my mood or anything. Sometimes I come home quiet, but sometimes I’m just tired. So… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed being down most days, exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel these concentration problems started because of the stress of your daughter moving back home?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it was because of her moving back in, exactly. I mean, that’s stressful, sure—anybody coming back into the house changes the whole rhythm, and then there’s groceries and noise and where everyone parks in the driveway...\n\nBut the thing that really got me to come in is, for months now, my mind just keeps jumping. Like I’ll be driving and every CB chirp, every fuel price sign, some random idea pops in my head, and I’m off track. Then I miss stuff I normally wouldn’t miss—like a client’s delivery note, or I forget who I told I’d call back. That’s not really like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tense or keyed up much of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… tense, or keyed up, I guess that fits more than “tense” exactly. It’s like I can’t just sit there. If I get home and sit on the couch, I start thinking, well, the route plans could be cleaned up, or maybe I should check on one of the newer guys and see if he figured out that dock entrance—because if you miss that turn, you end up circling forever, and I’ve done that before.\n\nAnd then at home I’ll start fixing little things, like a cabinet hinge or messing with something in the garage that didn’t need to be messed with right then. My wife will say, “Can you just sit down?” and I mean, I try, but I get antsy pretty quick. So yeah, I’ve been keeping myself busy with extra stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about a lot of different things?\n\nPatient: Excessively… I don’t know. I mean, I worry about normal stuff, I guess—work, bills, whether the truck’s making that sound again, because one time I ignored a little rattle and it turned into a whole ordeal at a rest stop outside Toledo.\n\nBut “a lot of different things” like all the time? I’m not sure I’d say that. Sometimes my mind goes from one thing to another, but I don’t know if I’d call it worrying exactly. It’s more like… I notice something and then I’m on to the next thing. So, I guess I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or low a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Sad or low… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say that’s been the main thing, no. I mean, everybody has days where they’re worn out or kind of fed up—like if traffic’s backed up for no reason and you’re sitting there watching the clock, that’ll put anybody in a mood.\n\nBut sad a lot lately? I haven’t really thought of it that way. My wife might say I’m distracted or hard to pin down, but low… I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: On most days, do you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Anxious… on most days, I’m not sure I’d put that word on it. Like, some days I’ve got that wound-up feeling, sure, especially if there’s stuff that needs doing, but anxious makes me think of, I don’t know, sitting there scared something bad’s about to happen.\n\nI’m more just… moving around, thinking of the next thing. Like if I’m on a route and I know there’s construction near the depot, I’ll start figuring out three different ways around it, but that’s kind of just how you do the job, you know? So… I don’t really know. Maybe sometimes, but I haven’t been thinking “I’m anxious” most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying your usual activities less than you used to?\n\nPatient: Enjoying things less… I don’t know, I haven’t really measured it like that. I haven’t noticed that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have these problems mainly been a reaction to your daughter moving back home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… that’s probably when it really started, around when she moved back in. My wife might say I’m distracted or hard to pin down, but low… I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: On most days, do you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Anxious… on most days, I’m not sure I’d put that word on it. Like, some days I’ve got that wound-up feeling, sure, especially if there’s stuff that needs doing, but anxious makes me think of, I don’t know, sitting there scared something bad’s about to happen.\n\nI’m more just… moving around, thinking of the next thing. Like if I’m on a route and I know there’s construction near the depot, I’ll start figuring out three different ways around it, but that’s kind of just how you do the job, you know? So… I don’t really know. Maybe sometimes, but I haven’t been thinking “I’m anxious” most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying your usual activities less than you used to?\n\nPatient: Enjoying things less… I don’t know, I haven’t really measured it like that. I still do the usual stuff when it comes up, you know? Watch a game if it’s on, mess around in the garage a bit, talk to the guys at work when there’s time.\n\nSometimes I get sidetracked before I even sit down to do something—like I’ll go out to clean up the workbench and then notice the leaf blower needs gas, and then I’m thinking about the mower belt, and next thing you know I didn’t actually do the thing I went out there for. But enjoying it less… I’m not sure. I haven’t noticed that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have these problems mainly been a reaction to your daughter moving back home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… that’s probably when it really started, around when she moved back in. It’s been, what, about seven months now.\n\nI wouldn’t say it’s just one thing, though. When someone moves back into the house, even family, everything shifts—routines, groceries, who’s using the washer, little stuff like that. I’ve just been trying to keep things steady, keep everybody from getting worked up. So yeah, it kind of lines up with that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Hard to control it… I don’t know. I guess it depends what you mean by worrying. If I’m thinking about stuff that needs fixing, I can usually just go do something else, or I’ll make a mental list.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I guess what finally made me come in is I’ve been getting real scattered lately. Like, I’m out driving and my mind jumps at every little thing — CB chatter, a fuel price sign, some random idea I get — and then I miss stuff I normally wouldn’t.\n\nI’ve been doing this kind of work a long time, you know, deliveries and clients and keeping track of who needs what, and usually I’m pretty solid. But lately I’ll miss a delivery note or forget who I said I’d call back. The other day I was thinking about fuel prices — they’ve been all over the place, and everybody’s got an opinion about them — and then I realized I’d overlooked something on a client’s paperwork. That’s not really like me, so… yeah, that’s what pushed me to come talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it depressed, exactly. I mean, everybody gets a little worn down, right? Especially with work being the way it is — long days, people needing things yesterday, traffic backed up for no reason. \n\nBut down like… sad all the time? I’m not sure. I haven’t really stopped to think of it that way. I’ve mostly just been trying to keep up with things.\n\nDoctor: Have you had this kind of trouble paying attention since you were a child?\n\nPatient: No, not really since I was a kid. I mean, I wasn’t the best student or anything — high school was high school, you know, teachers always had their way of doing things — but this kind of trouble feels more recent.\n\nI’d say it’s been maybe… seven months or so. Around when my daughter moved back home. Since then I’ve been trying to keep everything steady, you know, work and home and all that, and that’s when I started noticing I wasn’t tracking things like I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Worrying a lot… I don’t know. I mean, I think about stuff, sure. Work stuff, fuel, whether a customer’s gonna be annoyed if something’s late — but that’s kind of the job, you know?\n\nLike yesterday I was sitting there looking at the gas prices and thinking how they used to be way lower, and then you start thinking about which routes make more sense, and then somebody on the CB is talking nonsense and it’s just… it piles up. But worry-worry? I’m not sure I’d call it that. It’s more like my head keeps going from one thing to the next.\n\nDoctor: Have you been drinking more alcohol lately?\n\nPatient: Drinking more… I don’t know. I’ll have a beer now and then, same as a lot of guys, especially after a long day. But I haven’t really been keeping score or anything.\n\nFunny thing is, some places you deliver to, there’s always a bar right nearby, and the same trucks parked out front every week. I see that and think, well, people have their routines. Me, I’m usually just trying to get home or beat the traffic. So… I’m not sure I’d say more lately.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times after drinking when you couldn’t remember what happened?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not like waking up somewhere and not knowing how I got there, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere’s been nights where I’m tired and I’ll forget where I put my keys or what I was gonna do next, but that happens after a long route too. My buddy used to joke that half the stuff we forget is just because we’re getting older, not because of beer. So… no, I haven’t noticed anything like that after drinking, not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Has your drinking ever caused problems at work or at home?\n\nPatient: At work or home? Not that I can really point to. I mean, my wife might say I’m annoying if I leave a bottle cap on the counter or something, but that’s not exactly a “problem,” you know?\n\nWork-wise, I don’t drink on the job. With driving, you can’t mess around with that. I’ve seen guys lose routes over stupid choices, and once your record’s got a mark on it, good luck getting decent work again. So… no, I don’t think drinking has caused trouble like that for me.\n\nDoctor: Over the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling down most days?\n\nPatient: Down most days… I don’t know if I’d put it like that. I mean, everybody gets worn out, right? Especially when you’re on the road and traffic’s backed up and dispatch is calling like you can magically move cars out of the way.\n\nThese last couple weeks, I’ve had some off days, sure, but I haven’t really been keeping a chart of my mood or anything. Sometimes I come home quiet, but sometimes I’m just tired. So… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed being down most days, exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel these concentration problems started because of the stress of your daughter moving back home?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it was because of her moving back in, exactly. I mean, that’s stressful, sure—anybody coming back into the house changes the whole rhythm, and then there’s groceries and noise and where everyone parks in the driveway...\n\nBut the thing that really got me to come in is, for months now, my mind just keeps jumping. Like I’ll be driving and every CB chirp, every fuel price sign, some random idea pops in my head, and I’m off track. Then I miss stuff I normally wouldn’t miss—like a client’s delivery note, or I forget who I told I’d call back. That’s not really like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tense or keyed up much of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… tense, or keyed up, I guess that fits more than “tense” exactly. It’s like I can’t just sit there. If I get home and sit on the couch, I start thinking, well, the route plans could be cleaned up, or maybe I should check on one of the newer guys and see if he figured out that dock entrance—because if you miss that turn, you end up circling forever, and I’ve done that before.\n\nAnd then at home I’ll start fixing little things, like a cabinet hinge or messing with something in the garage that didn’t need to be messed with right then. My wife will say, “Can you just sit down?” and I mean, I try, but I get antsy pretty quick. So yeah, I’ve been keeping myself busy with extra stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about a lot of different things?\n\nPatient: Excessively… I don’t know. I mean, I worry about normal stuff, I guess—work, bills, whether the truck’s making that sound again, because one time I ignored a little rattle and it turned into a whole ordeal at a rest stop outside Toledo.\n\nBut “a lot of different things” like all the time? I’m not sure I’d say that. Sometimes my mind goes from one thing to another, but I don’t know if I’d call it worrying exactly. It’s more like… I notice something and then I’m on to the next thing. So, I guess I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or low a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Sad or low… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say that’s been the main thing, no. I mean, everybody has days where they’re worn out or kind of fed up—like if traffic’s backed up for no reason and you’re sitting there watching the clock, that’ll put anybody in a mood.\n\nBut sad a lot lately? I haven’t really thought of it that way. My buddy used to joke that half the stuff we forget is just because we’re getting older, not because of beer. So… no, I haven’t noticed anything like that after drinking, not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Has your drinking ever caused problems at work or at home?\n\nPatient: At work or home? Not that I can really point to. I mean, my wife might say I’m annoying if I leave a bottle cap on the counter or something, but that’s not exactly a “problem,” you know?\n\nWork-wise, I don’t drink on the job. With driving, you can’t mess around with that. I’ve seen guys lose routes over stupid choices, and once your record’s got a mark on it, good luck getting decent work again. So… no, I don’t think drinking has caused trouble like that for me.\n\nDoctor: Over the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling down most days?\n\nPatient: Down most days… I don’t know if I’d put it like that. I mean, everybody gets worn out, right? Especially when you’re on the road and traffic’s backed up and dispatch is calling like you can magically move cars out of the way.\n\nThese last couple weeks, I’ve had some off days, sure, but I haven’t really been keeping a chart of my mood or anything. Sometimes I come home quiet, but sometimes I’m just tired. So… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed being down most days, exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel these concentration problems started because of the stress of your daughter moving back home?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it was because of her moving back in, exactly. I mean, that’s stressful, sure—anybody coming back into the house changes the whole rhythm, and then there’s groceries and noise and where everyone parks in the driveway...\n\nBut the thing that really got me to come in is, for months now, my mind just keeps jumping. Like I’ll be driving and every CB chirp, every fuel price sign, some random idea pops in my head, and I’m off track. Then I miss stuff I normally wouldn’t miss—like a client’s delivery note, or I forget who I told I’d call back. That’s not really like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tense or keyed up much of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… tense, or keyed up, I guess that fits more than “tense” exactly. It’s like I can’t just sit there. If I get home and sit on the couch, I start thinking, well, the route plans could be cleaned up, or maybe I should check on one of the newer guys and see if he figured out that dock entrance—because if you miss that turn, you end up circling forever, and I’ve done that before.\n\nAnd then at home I’ll start fixing little things, like a cabinet hinge or messing with something in the garage that didn’t need to be messed with right then. My wife will say, “Can you just sit down?” and I mean, I try, but I get antsy pretty quick. So yeah, I’ve been keeping myself busy with extra stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about a lot of different things?\n\nPatient: Excessively… I don’t know. I mean, I worry about normal stuff, I guess—work, bills, whether the truck’s making that sound again, because one time I ignored a little rattle and it turned into a whole ordeal at a rest stop outside Toledo.\n\nBut “a lot of different things” like all the time? I’m not sure I’d say that. Sometimes my mind goes from one thing to another, but I don’t know if I’d call it worrying exactly. It’s more like… I notice something and then I’m on to the next thing. So, I guess I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or low a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Sad or low… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say that’s been the main thing, no. I mean, everybody has days where they’re worn out or kind of fed up—like if traffic’s backed up for no reason and you’re sitting there watching the clock, that’ll put anybody in a mood.\n\nBut sad a lot lately? I haven’t really thought of it that way. My wife might say I’m distracted or hard to pin down, but low… I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: On most days, do you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Anxious… on most days, I’m not sure I’d put that word on it. Like, some days I’ve got that wound-up feeling, sure, especially if there’s stuff that needs doing, but anxious makes me think of, I don’t know, sitting there scared something bad’s about to happen.\n\nI’m more just… moving around, thinking of the next thing. Like if I’m on a route and I know there’s construction near the depot, I’ll start figuring out three different ways around it, but that’s kind of just how you do the job, you know? So… I don’t really know. Maybe sometimes, but I haven’t been thinking “I’m anxious” most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying your usual activities less than you used to?\n\nPatient: Enjoying things less… I don’t know, I haven’t really measured it like that. I still do the usual stuff when it comes up, you know? Watch a game if it’s on, mess around in the garage a bit, talk to the guys at work when there’s time.\n\nSometimes I get sidetracked before I even sit down to do something—like I’ll go out to clean up the workbench and then notice the leaf blower needs gas, and then I’m thinking about the mower belt, and next thing you know I didn’t actually do the thing I went out there for. But enjoying it less… I’m not sure. I haven’t noticed that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have these problems mainly been a reaction to your daughter moving back home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… that’s probably when it really started, around when she moved back in. It’s been, what, about seven months now.\n\nI wouldn’t say it’s just one thing, though. When someone moves back into the house, even family, everything shifts—routines, groceries, who’s using the washer, little stuff like that. I’ve just been trying to keep things steady, keep everybody from getting worked up. So yeah, it kind of lines up with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to control your worrying?\n\nPatient: Hard to control it… I don’t know. I guess it depends what you mean by worrying. If I’m thinking about stuff that needs fixing, I can usually just go do something else, or I’ll make a mental list.\n\nLike with the truck, if there’s a noise, you don’t ignore it, you listen for it, you know? But then sometimes it turns out to be nothing, like a loose strap or whatever. So I don’t know if that’s worrying or just paying attention.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’m sitting there unable to stop it all the time. Not that I’ve noticed, anyway.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… I mean, I’d like to say no, but it probably has.

At work, I’ve had dispatch repeat things that I should’ve caught the first time—addresses, times, little details. And with clients too, sometimes they’ll tell me something and then I’m halfway thinking about another route or some idea I had for how to organize the day better, and then I’m asking them again like I wasn’t listening. That’s not usually me.

And I’ve had a couple sales follow-ups slip through, which bothers me. I’ll start chasing one thing, then another, and it’s like when you’re cleaning out the garage and find a box of old parts—next thing you know you’ve got five things open and none finished. Same kind of deal.

At home… yeah, there’s some strain. My wife and daughter, I can tell sometimes they’re kind of watching what they say, like walking on eggshells. I’m supposed to be helping keep things settled, and instead I think I add to the tension sometimes.

So yeah, it kind of lines up with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to control your worrying?\n\nPatient: Hard to control it… I don’t know. I guess it depends what you mean by worrying. If I’m thinking about stuff that needs fixing, I can usually just go do something else, or I’ll make a mental list.\n\nLike with the truck, if there’s a noise, you don’t ignore it, you listen for it, you know? But then sometimes it turns out to be nothing, like a loose strap or whatever. So I don’t know if that’s worrying or just paying attention.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’m sitting there unable to stop it all the time. Not that I’ve noticed, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have these concentration and restless feelings been causing problems in your work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’d like to say no, but it probably has.\n\nAt work, I’ve had dispatch repeat things that I should’ve caught the first time—addresses, times, little details. And with clients too, sometimes they’ll tell me something and then I’m halfway thinking about another route or some idea I had for how to organize the day better, and then I’m asking them again like I wasn’t listening. That’s not usually me.\n\nAnd I’ve had a couple sales follow-ups slip through, which bothers me. I’ll start chasing one thing, then another, and it’s like when you’re cleaning out the garage and find a box of old parts—next thing you know you’ve got five things open and none finished. Same kind of deal.\n\nAt home… yeah, there’s some strain. My wife and daughter, I can tell sometimes they’re kind of watching what they say, like walking on eggshells. Most days? I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, everybody’s a little keyed up depending on what’s going on.

Like yesterday, traffic was backed up near the bridge, and that’ll get anybody on edge because then the whole schedule starts sliding, and then you’re thinking about who’s waiting and whether dispatch is gonna call. But once you get through it, it’s just… you keep moving.

Since then I’ve been trying to keep everything steady, you know, work and home and all that, and that’s when I started noticing I wasn’t tracking things like I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Worrying a lot… I don’t know. I mean, I think about stuff, sure. Work stuff, fuel, whether a customer’s gonna be annoyed if something’s late — but that’s kind of the job, you know?\n\nLike yesterday I was sitting there looking at the gas prices and thinking how they used to be way lower, and then you start thinking about which routes make more sense, and then somebody on the CB is talking nonsense and it’s just… it piles up. But worry-worry? I’m not sure I’d call it that. It’s more like my head keeps going from one thing to the next.\n\nDoctor: Have you been drinking more alcohol lately?\n\nPatient: Drinking more… I don’t know. I’ll have a beer now and then, same as a lot of guys, especially after a long day. But I haven’t really been keeping score or anything.\n\nFunny thing is, some places you deliver to, there’s always a bar right nearby, and the same trucks parked out front every week. I see that and think, well, people have their routines. Me, I’m usually just trying to get home or beat the traffic. So… I’m not sure I’d say more lately.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times after drinking when you couldn’t remember what happened?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not like waking up somewhere and not knowing how I got there, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere’s been nights where I’m tired and I’ll forget where I put my keys or what I was gonna do next, but that happens after a long route too. My buddy used to joke that half the stuff we forget is just because we’re getting older, not because of beer. So… no, I haven’t noticed anything like that after drinking, not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Has your drinking ever caused problems at work or at home?\n\nPatient: At work or home? Not that I can really point to. I mean, my wife might say I’m annoying if I leave a bottle cap on the counter or something, but that’s not exactly a “problem,” you know?\n\nWork-wise, I don’t drink on the job. With driving, you can’t mess around with that. I’ve seen guys lose routes over stupid choices, and once your record’s got a mark on it, good luck getting decent work again. So… no, I don’t think drinking has caused trouble like that for me.\n\nDoctor: Over the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling down most days?\n\nPatient: Down most days… I don’t know if I’d put it like that. I mean, everybody gets worn out, right? Especially when you’re on the road and traffic’s backed up and dispatch is calling like you can magically move cars out of the way.\n\nThese last couple weeks, I’ve had some off days, sure, but I haven’t really been keeping a chart of my mood or anything. Sometimes I come home quiet, but sometimes I’m just tired. So… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed being down most days, exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel these concentration problems started because of the stress of your daughter moving back home?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it was because of her moving back in, exactly. I mean, that’s stressful, sure—anybody coming back into the house changes the whole rhythm, and then there’s groceries and noise and where everyone parks in the driveway...\n\nBut the thing that really got me to come in is, for months now, my mind just keeps jumping. Like I’ll be driving and every CB chirp, every fuel price sign, some random idea pops in my head, and I’m off track. Then I miss stuff I normally wouldn’t miss—like a client’s delivery note, or I forget who I told I’d call back. That’s not really like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tense or keyed up much of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… tense, or keyed up, I guess that fits more than “tense” exactly. It’s like I can’t just sit there. If I get home and sit on the couch, I start thinking, well, the route plans could be cleaned up, or maybe I should check on one of the newer guys and see if he figured out that dock entrance—because if you miss that turn, you end up circling forever, and I’ve done that before.\n\nAnd then at home I’ll start fixing little things, like a cabinet hinge or messing with something in the garage that didn’t need to be messed with right then. My wife will say, “Can you just sit down?” and I mean, I try, but I get antsy pretty quick. So yeah, I’ve been keeping myself busy with extra stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about a lot of different things?\n\nPatient: Excessively… I don’t know. I mean, I worry about normal stuff, I guess—work, bills, whether the truck’s making that sound again, because one time I ignored a little rattle and it turned into a whole ordeal at a rest stop outside Toledo.\n\nBut “a lot of different things” like all the time? I’m not sure I’d say that. Sometimes my mind goes from one thing to another, but I don’t know if I’d call it worrying exactly. It’s more like… I notice something and then I’m on to the next thing. So, I guess I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or low a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Sad or low… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say that’s been the main thing, no. I mean, everybody has days where they’re worn out or kind of fed up—like if traffic’s backed up for no reason and you’re sitting there watching the clock, that’ll put anybody in a mood.\n\nBut sad a lot lately? I haven’t really thought of it that way. My wife might say I’m distracted or hard to pin down, but low… I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: On most days, do you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Anxious… on most days, I’m not sure I’d put that word on it. Like, some days I’ve got that wound-up feeling, sure, especially if there’s stuff that needs doing, but anxious makes me think of, I don’t know, sitting there scared something bad’s about to happen.\n\nI’m more just… moving around, thinking of the next thing. Like if I’m on a route and I know there’s construction near the depot, I’ll start figuring out three different ways around it, but that’s kind of just how you do the job, you know? So… I don’t really know. Maybe sometimes, but I haven’t been thinking “I’m anxious” most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying your usual activities less than you used to?\n\nPatient: Enjoying things less… I don’t know, I haven’t really measured it like that. I still do the usual stuff when it comes up, you know? Watch a game if it’s on, mess around in the garage a bit, talk to the guys at work when there’s time.\n\nSometimes I get sidetracked before I even sit down to do something—like I’ll go out to clean up the workbench and then notice the leaf blower needs gas, and then I’m thinking about the mower belt, and next thing you know I didn’t actually do the thing I went out there for. But enjoying it less… I’m not sure. I haven’t noticed that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have these problems mainly been a reaction to your daughter moving back home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… that’s probably when it really started, around when she moved back in. It’s been, what, about seven months now.\n\nI wouldn’t say it’s just one thing, though. When someone moves back into the house, even family, everything shifts—routines, groceries, who’s using the washer, little stuff like that. I’ve just been trying to keep things steady, keep everybody from getting worked up. So yeah, it kind of lines up with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to control your worrying?\n\nPatient: Hard to control it… I don’t know. I guess it depends what you mean by worrying. If I’m thinking about stuff that needs fixing, I can usually just go do something else, or I’ll make a mental list.\n\nLike with the truck, if there’s a noise, you don’t ignore it, you listen for it, you know? But then sometimes it turns out to be nothing, like a loose strap or whatever. So I don’t know if that’s worrying or just paying attention.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’m sitting there unable to stop it all the time. Not that I’ve noticed, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have these concentration and restless feelings been causing problems in your work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’d like to say no, but it probably has.\n\nAt work, I’ve had dispatch repeat things that I should’ve caught the first time—addresses, times, little details. And with clients too, sometimes they’ll tell me something and then I’m halfway thinking about another route or some idea I had for how to organize the day better, and then I’m asking them again like I wasn’t listening. That’s not usually me.\n\nAnd I’ve had a couple sales follow-ups slip through, which bothers me. I’ll start chasing one thing, then another, and it’s like when you’re cleaning out the garage and find a box of old parts—next thing you know you’ve got five things open and none finished. Same kind of deal.\n\nAt home… yeah, there’s some strain. My wife and daughter, I can tell sometimes they’re kind of watching what they say, like walking on eggshells. I’m supposed to be helping keep things settled, and instead I think I add to the tension sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Over the past several months, have you felt nervous or on edge on most days?\n\nPatient: Most days? I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, everybody’s a little keyed up depending on what’s going on.\n\nLike yesterday, traffic was backed up near the bridge, and that’ll get anybody on edge because then the whole schedule starts sliding, and then you’re thinking about who’s waiting and whether dispatch is gonna call. But once you get through it, it’s just… you keep moving.\n\nSo, nervous? I don’t know. I haven’t really been thinking of myself as nervous every day or anything like that. More like just trying to stay on top of things, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [doctor] ==========
{"candidates":["F43.2","F41.1","F32.9"],"note":"","is_final":false}

Same kind of deal.\n\nAt home… yeah, there’s some strain. My wife and daughter, I can tell sometimes they’re kind of watching what they say, like walking on eggshells. I’m supposed to be helping keep things settled, and instead I think I add to the tension sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Over the past several months, have you felt nervous or on edge on most days?\n\nPatient: Most days? I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, everybody’s a little keyed up depending on what’s going on.\n\nLike yesterday, traffic was backed up near the bridge, and that’ll get anybody on edge because then the whole schedule starts sliding, and then you’re thinking about who’s waiting and whether dispatch is gonna call. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Maybe some of it, yeah. I mean, if she moved back out and the house wasn’t so… tight all the time, I’d probably breathe easier.

But I don’t know if it would just disappear overnight. This has been going on, what, about seven months now? Since she came back home and I started feeling like I had to keep everything steady, keep everybody from bumping into each other wrong, you know? It’s like when you’re hauling something that shifts in the back—you start listening for every little sound even after the load’s tied down.

So yeah, it’s connected to that, I think. But after seven months, it kind of becomes a habit too.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.2, F41.1, F32.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I guess what finally made me come in is I’ve been getting real scattered lately. Like, I’m out driving and my mind jumps at every little thing — CB chatter, a fuel price sign, some random idea I get — and then I miss stuff I normally wouldn’t.\n\nI’ve been doing this kind of work a long time, you know, deliveries and clients and keeping track of who needs what, and usually I’m pretty solid. But lately I’ll miss a delivery note or forget who I said I’d call back. The other day I was thinking about fuel prices — they’ve been all over the place, and everybody’s got an opinion about them — and then I realized I’d overlooked something on a client’s paperwork. That’s not really like me, so… yeah, that’s what pushed me to come talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it depressed, exactly. I mean, everybody gets a little worn down, right? Especially with work being the way it is — long days, people needing things yesterday, traffic backed up for no reason. \n\nBut down like… sad all the time? I’m not sure. I haven’t really stopped to think of it that way. I’ve mostly just been trying to keep up with things.\n\nDoctor: Have you had this kind of trouble paying attention since you were a child?\n\nPatient: No, not really since I was a kid. I mean, I wasn’t the best student or anything — high school was high school, you know, teachers always had their way of doing things — but this kind of trouble feels more recent.\n\nI’d say it’s been maybe… seven months or so. Around when my daughter moved back home. Since then I’ve been trying to keep everything steady, you know, work and home and all that, and that’s when I started noticing I wasn’t tracking things like I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Worrying a lot… I don’t know. I mean, I think about stuff, sure. Work stuff, fuel, whether a customer’s gonna be annoyed if something’s late — but that’s kind of the job, you know?\n\nLike yesterday I was sitting there looking at the gas prices and thinking how they used to be way lower, and then you start thinking about which routes make more sense, and then somebody on the CB is talking nonsense and it’s just… it piles up. But worry-worry? I’m not sure I’d call it that. It’s more like my head keeps going from one thing to the next.\n\nDoctor: Have you been drinking more alcohol lately?\n\nPatient: Drinking more… I don’t know. I’ll have a beer now and then, same as a lot of guys, especially after a long day. But I haven’t really been keeping score or anything.\n\nFunny thing is, some places you deliver to, there’s always a bar right nearby, and the same trucks parked out front every week. I see that and think, well, people have their routines. Me, I’m usually just trying to get home or beat the traffic. So… I’m not sure I’d say more lately.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times after drinking when you couldn’t remember what happened?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not like waking up somewhere and not knowing how I got there, if that’s what you mean.\n\nThere’s been nights where I’m tired and I’ll forget where I put my keys or what I was gonna do next, but that happens after a long route too. My buddy used to joke that half the stuff we forget is just because we’re getting older, not because of beer. So… no, I haven’t noticed anything like that after drinking, not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Has your drinking ever caused problems at work or at home?\n\nPatient: At work or home? Not that I can really point to. I mean, my wife might say I’m annoying if I leave a bottle cap on the counter or something, but that’s not exactly a “problem,” you know?\n\nWork-wise, I don’t drink on the job. With driving, you can’t mess around with that. I’ve seen guys lose routes over stupid choices, and once your record’s got a mark on it, good luck getting decent work again. So… no, I don’t think drinking has caused trouble like that for me.\n\nDoctor: Over the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling down most days?\n\nPatient: Down most days… I don’t know if I’d put it like that. I mean, everybody gets worn out, right? Especially when you’re on the road and traffic’s backed up and dispatch is calling like you can magically move cars out of the way.\n\nThese last couple weeks, I’ve had some off days, sure, but I haven’t really been keeping a chart of my mood or anything. Sometimes I come home quiet, but sometimes I’m just tired. So… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed being down most days, exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel these concentration problems started because of the stress of your daughter moving back home?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it was because of her moving back in, exactly. I mean, that’s stressful, sure—anybody coming back into the house changes the whole rhythm, and then there’s groceries and noise and where everyone parks in the driveway...\n\nBut the thing that really got me to come in is, for months now, my mind just keeps jumping. Like I’ll be driving and every CB chirp, every fuel price sign, some random idea pops in my head, and I’m off track. Then I miss stuff I normally wouldn’t miss—like a client’s delivery note, or I forget who I told I’d call back. That’s not really like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tense or keyed up much of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… tense, or keyed up, I guess that fits more than “tense” exactly. It’s like I can’t just sit there. If I get home and sit on the couch, I start thinking, well, the route plans could be cleaned up, or maybe I should check on one of the newer guys and see if he figured out that dock entrance—because if you miss that turn, you end up circling forever, and I’ve done that before.\n\nAnd then at home I’ll start fixing little things, like a cabinet hinge or messing with something in the garage that didn’t need to be messed with right then. My wife will say, “Can you just sit down?” and I mean, I try, but I get antsy pretty quick. So yeah, I’ve been keeping myself busy with extra stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about a lot of different things?\n\nPatient: Excessively… I don’t know. I mean, I worry about normal stuff, I guess—work, bills, whether the truck’s making that sound again, because one time I ignored a little rattle and it turned into a whole ordeal at a rest stop outside Toledo.\n\nBut “a lot of different things” like all the time? I’m not sure I’d say that. Sometimes my mind goes from one thing to another, but I don’t know if I’d call it worrying exactly. It’s more like… I notice something and then I’m on to the next thing. So, I guess I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or low a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Sad or low… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say that’s been the main thing, no. I mean, everybody has days where they’re worn out or kind of fed up—like if traffic’s backed up for no reason and you’re sitting there watching the clock, that’ll put anybody in a mood.\n\nBut sad a lot lately? I haven’t really thought of it that way. My wife might say I’m distracted or hard to pin down, but low… I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: On most days, do you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Anxious… on most days, I’m not sure I’d put that word on it. Like, some days I’ve got that wound-up feeling, sure, especially if there’s stuff that needs doing, but anxious makes me think of, I don’t know, sitting there scared something bad’s about to happen.\n\nI’m more just… moving around, thinking of the next thing. Like if I’m on a route and I know there’s construction near the depot, I’ll start figuring out three different ways around it, but that’s kind of just how you do the job, you know? So… I don’t really know. Maybe sometimes, but I haven’t been thinking “I’m anxious” most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying your usual activities less than you used to?\n\nPatient: Enjoying things less… I don’t know, I haven’t really measured it like that. I still do the usual stuff when it comes up, you know? Watch a game if it’s on, mess around in the garage a bit, talk to the guys at work when there’s time.\n\nSometimes I get sidetracked before I even sit down to do something—like I’ll go out to clean up the workbench and then notice the leaf blower needs gas, and then I’m thinking about the mower belt, and next thing you know I didn’t actually do the thing I went out there for. But enjoying it less… I’m not sure. I haven’t noticed that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have these problems mainly been a reaction to your daughter moving back home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… that’s probably when it really started, around when she moved back in. It’s been, what, about seven months now.\n\nI wouldn’t say it’s just one thing, though. When someone moves back into the house, even family, everything shifts—routines, groceries, who’s using the washer, little stuff like that. I’ve just been trying to keep things steady, keep everybody from getting worked up. So yeah, it kind of lines up with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to control your worrying?\n\nPatient: Hard to control it… I don’t know. I guess it depends what you mean by worrying. If I’m thinking about stuff that needs fixing, I can usually just go do something else, or I’ll make a mental list.\n\nLike with the truck, if there’s a noise, you don’t ignore it, you listen for it, you know? But then sometimes it turns out to be nothing, like a loose strap or whatever. Maybe sometimes, but I haven’t been thinking “I’m anxious” most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying your usual activities less than you used to?\n\nPatient: Enjoying things less… I don’t know, I haven’t really measured it like that. I still do the usual stuff when it comes up, you know? Watch a game if it’s on, mess around in the garage a bit, talk to the guys at work when there’s time.\n\nSometimes I get sidetracked before I even sit down to do something—like I’ll go out to clean up the workbench and then notice the leaf blower needs gas, and then I’m thinking about the mower belt, and next thing you know I didn’t actually do the thing I went out there for. But enjoying it less… I’m not sure. I haven’t noticed that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have these problems mainly been a reaction to your daughter moving back home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… that’s probably when it really started, around when she moved back in. It’s been, what, about seven months now.\n\nI wouldn’t say it’s just one thing, though. When someone moves back into the house, even family, everything shifts—routines, groceries, who’s using the washer, little stuff like that. I’ve just been trying to keep things steady, keep everybody from getting worked up. So yeah, it kind of lines up with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to control your worrying?\n\nPatient: Hard to control it… I don’t know. I guess it depends what you mean by worrying. If I’m thinking about stuff that needs fixing, I can usually just go do something else, or I’ll make a mental list.\n\nLike with the truck, if there’s a noise, you don’t ignore it, you listen for it, you know? But then sometimes it turns out to be nothing, like a loose strap or whatever. So I don’t know if that’s worrying or just paying attention.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’m sitting there unable to stop it all the time. Not that I’ve noticed, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have these concentration and restless feelings been causing problems in your work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’d like to say no, but it probably has.\n\nAt work, I’ve had dispatch repeat things that I should’ve caught the first time—addresses, times, little details. And with clients too, sometimes they’ll tell me something and then I’m halfway thinking about another route or some idea I had for how to organize the day better, and then I’m asking them again like I wasn’t listening. That’s not usually me.\n\nAnd I’ve had a couple sales follow-ups slip through, which bothers me. I’ll start chasing one thing, then another, and it’s like when you’re cleaning out the garage and find a box of old parts—next thing you know you’ve got five things open and none finished. Same kind of deal.\n\nAt home… yeah, there’s some strain. My wife and daughter, I can tell sometimes they’re kind of watching what they say, like walking on eggshells. I’m supposed to be helping keep things settled, and instead I think I add to the tension sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Over the past several months, have you felt nervous or on edge on most days?\n\nPatient: Most days? I’m not sure I’d put it that way. I mean, everybody’s a little keyed up depending on what’s going on.\n\nLike yesterday, traffic was backed up near the bridge, and that’ll get anybody on edge because then the whole schedule starts sliding, and then you’re thinking about who’s waiting and whether dispatch is gonna call. But once you get through it, it’s just… you keep moving.\n\nSo, nervous? I don’t know. I haven’t really been thinking of myself as nervous every day or anything like that. More like just trying to stay on top of things, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: If your daughter moved back out and things at home settled down, do you think these concentration and restless feelings would mostly go away?\n\nPatient: Maybe some of it, yeah. I mean, if she moved back out and the house wasn’t so… tight all the time, I’d probably breathe easier.\n\nBut I don’t know if it would just disappear overnight. This has been going on, what, about seven months now? Since she came back home and I started feeling like I had to keep everything steady, keep everybody from bumping into each other wrong, you know? It’s like when you’re hauling something that shifts in the back—you start listening for every little sound even after the load’s tied down.\n\nSo yeah, it’s connected to that, I think.
